Harvey to Bring Jus Mundi’s Arbitration Agent Into Legal Workflows

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Harvey announced on November 4, 2026 a partnership to integrate Jus Mundi’s Jus AI arbitration agent into its Assistant, Agents, Vault and Workflows products. The integration is intended for international arbitration and cross-border disputes teams, with global availability to joint customers planned by the end of 2026 at no additional cost.

The integration is designed to place specialist research beside the matter record. Once activated, a lawyer evaluating a client’s position could draw on applicable awards, treaty practice and institutional rules, then test the resulting research against documents in the matter’s Harvey Vault. The matter remains in Harvey, and findings can be carried into a memo, pleading or witness-preparation outline. Each authority links back to its source document on Jus Mundi’s platform.

Those results can be reviewed in the context of the matter’s pleadings, exhibits and transcripts. Pinpoint citations support checking the underlying material before an answer is presented to a tribunal. Recurring disputes work covered by the integration includes preparing a Request for Arbitration, checking authorities while drafting, comparing exhibit bundles with precedent and identifying clauses that rely on superseded international rules.

Jus Mundi’s source base spans investment and commercial awards, decisions, treaties, procedural materials and publications. Content relationships include the IBA and arbitral institutions including the ICC, AAA-ICDR, LCIA, SIAC, HKIAC, DIAC and Court of Arbitration for Sport. Secondary materials include the ICC Dispute Resolution Bulletin, ICSID Review and Redfern and Hunter on International Arbitration.

Jus Mundi’s Jus AI product page says Jus Mundi Data contains more than 125,000 case documents, awards and leading publications through more than 110 exclusive institutional and publisher partnerships. Its My Sources feature can search and analyze up to 10,000 user documents, with files secured and encrypted in a private Vault.

Jus AI runs on Jus AI Tenet V5, which Jus Mundi describes as a proprietary large language model trained on arbitration and international law. Users can choose four reasoning modes — Auto, Light, Think and Deep — and inspect reasoning steps, check citations and follow links back to sources.

The integration will operate within Harvey’s existing security, confidentiality and data-governance controls. Jus Mundi separately lists ISO 27001 and ISO 42001 certifications and a SOC 2 Type II attestation for Jus AI. The security provisions are central to a workflow that combines outside research with confidential case materials and private user documents.

Harvey Chief Business Officer John Haddock said the partnership responds to clients seeking deep domain expertise inside the platform where they manage important matters. Emmanuel Jacomy, a partner at A&O Shearman, said bringing the tools together would give arbitration teams a new way of working, with more time for client advocacy and strategic tasks.

For arbitration teams, the key change is where the work happens: source research, citation checking and drafting are designed to share one matter context, reducing the handoffs between a specialist research platform and the case file.

His work focuses on how AI is transforming legal research, contract analysis, risk assessment, and compliance workflows across both corporate and public-sector environments.

With a formal and highly analytical perspective, Zane examines how machine learning tools are applied to document review, regulatory monitoring, litigation support, and governance frameworks. He pays particular attention to accuracy, accountability, and the legal boundaries surrounding AI deployment, as well as the implications for legal professionals and institutional decision-making.

Florida Voters Approve Landmark Measure To Reform Property Taxes

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Florida voters are projected to approve a measure that would reform the state's property tax system, increasing the homestead property tax exemption and reducing the tax bill for some households.

Amendment 3, championed by outgoing Gov. Ron DeSantis, aims to increase the homestead property tax exemption from $50,000 to $150,000 in 2027 and to $250,000 in 2028. DeSantis, a Republican, estimated the latter threshold would eliminate property tax for 60% of Florida homeowners.

At 9:45 p.m. ET, with 98% of votes reported, The Associated Press reported the measure had 62% of votes in favor and 38% opposed. The measure, which would go into effect in 2027, needs 60% voter approval to pass.

"Big win on Amendment 3 for Florida homeowners," DeSantis posted on X.

The exemption would apply only to Floridians who established residency prior to December 2026. New residents would need to wait five years before being eligible for the exemptions.

The measure would also lower the annual cap on assessment increases for nonhomestead properties to 5%, down from the current 10%.

Byron Donalds, a GOP representative projected to win the election to replace the term-limited DeSantis, supported the measure. His opponent, Democrat David Jolly, did not. AP projects that Donalds will win the governor's race.

"In a state where homeowners insurance premiums are growing quickly due to climate concerns, which in turn drives HOA dues higher as well, any relief from the annual carrying costs of owning a home in Florida is welcome," says Realtor.com senior economist Joel Berner.

Berner notes that the median listing price in Florida fell 3.5% year over year in September and the number of homes for sale fell 9.6% from last year.

"Buyers and sellers are opting out of participation in the market as affordability constraints keep deals from going the distance at prices both parties can agree upon," the economist says.

On Tuesday night, Florida Realtors® President Chuck Bonfiglio Jr. said the result proved "Florida homeowners chose property tax relief."

"At a time when families are paying more for housing, insurance, groceries and everyday necessities, voters seized the opportunity to keep more of their own hard-earned money, taking a meaningful step toward making Florida a more affordable place to buy, own and keep a home," he said in a statement to Realtor.com.

"Florida Realtors was proud to stand with homeowners in supporting Amendment 3. We have worked with state and local leaders for decades to reduce barriers to homeownership, and we look forward to building on that partnership as Florida puts this relief into place responsibly, without compromising the essential services our communities depend on.”

The MidFlorida Credit Union estimated in 2024 that the average homeowner in the state pays around $2,338 a year in property taxes. In more expensive areas like Miami-Dade County and Palm Beach County, taxes can be significantly higher.

DeSantis argued that the measure would provide much needed financial relief for homeowners. Opponents argued the reduction in property tax revenue would have a deleterious effect on local governments.

Florida does not have an individual income tax or estate taxes, creating a limited pool of money for local governments to fund with. State economists had argued that the measure would wipe out nearly a quarter of all funding for local governments and deprive schools of billions in local revenue, hitting smaller counties especially hard.

In August, a circuit court judge ruled that the bill's original title, "Save Our Homes From Excessive Property Taxes," was "clearly and conclusively defective" and "more akin to a political slogan" than a neutral statement. The judge also dinged the bill for including "factually inaccurate" language and ordered that ballot language be amended prior to the midterm election.

Florida Attorney General James Uthmeier then changed the name of the proposed amendment to the "Homestead Tax Exemptions, Property Assessments, and Spending Restrictions Amendment" and revised the language that ultimately appeared on the ballot.

A model by the National Association of Realtors® found cutting property taxes by $200 a month lowered the income needed to afford the median-priced Florida home from about $133,800 to $125,200—a move that would put roughly 242,000 additional households above the affordability threshold.

NAR estimates that about 93,500 additional households under 45—including roughly 40,000 younger renters—could afford a starter home.

Florida homeowners might be thinking of more immediate relief, though. The state has some of the highest homeowners association fees in the country. It is also a center of the nation's property insurance crisis.

Florida Realtors' political action committee put $18 million into a "Vote Yes on 3" campaign, according to WPTV, significantly outraising its opponents. In a previously published op-ed to Realtor.com, Bonfiglio denied that the measure necessarily meant cuts to public services.

“Voting yes on Amendment 3 is Step 1, where Floridians decide that homeowners deserve this relief. But we can't forget about Step 2: implementation, where communities will work through how to deliver that relief while keeping public safety and core services first,” he wrote.

Meanwhile, opponents argue that such a measure would increase costs for public services, and that the measure privileges the desires of homeowners over non-homeowning citizens. An analysis published by the Florida Housing Coalition found that an estimated $1.61 billion in property taxes could be shifted to rental homes in 2028, growing to $2.19 billion by 2031.

"There's a small segment of homeowners that will benefit from this," Mike Weinstein, Jacksonville's chief administrative officer and a former state legislator, told NPR. "But the majority of people—renters, business owners, everyone else, and even many homeowners—won't see any benefit from this amendment at all."

Tristan Navera is a senior reporter on housing policy, covering trends and solutions in the housing market from Washington, DC. He was previously a senior reporter at Bloomberg Law, and before that covered real estate for the Washington Business Journal. Earlier in his career, he spent a decade reporting on business and real estate in Dayton and Columbus, OH. A Cincinnati native, he holds a journalism degree from Ohio University.

Giverny Capital Asset Management Q3 2026 Investment Letter

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

For the third quarter of the year ended September 30, 2026, the Giverny Capital Asset Management (âGCAMâ) model portfolio performed as follows:1

Returns longer than one year are annualized

We held our annual client meeting in New York City on October 9th, discussing the portfolio and taking questions from clients for two hours. As always, my colleagues Francois Rochon, John Bleday and I appreciated the excellent dialogue. Clients will receive a link to a video of the meeting soon. We hope you will take the time to watch as I think it would add to your understanding of our portfolio and philosophy. We also welcome your feedback on the usefulness of the event and video.

Our results continue to lag the market, but at the same time our companies overall are growing their earnings at an impressive clip. Over time, stock performance tends to track earnings growth. For now, we're faced with a very narrow market that can be dismissive of the future of businesses not strictly focused on Artificial Intelligence. At the end of the quarter, we had 23 stocks in the portfolio. Of those, two had outperformed the market for the year-to-date: Arista Networks (ANET)(+55%) and Taiwan Semiconductor (TSM)(+60%). The other 21 not only each returned less than the 12.75% generated by the Index, but on a cumulative basis were slightly negative.

This is either nuts or marks a new era in which investors accurately perceive that all global corporate earnings growth is going to accrue to a small handful of companies at the forefront of AI and the data center buildout. This may be true. I don't think it is, but if I am wrong, our portfolio may continue to lag the Index. To cite one extreme example of a stock that has become decoupled from business results, Charles Schwab (SCHW) stock is flat for the year even as its earnings are expected to grow 30% this year and another 25% or so next year. The stock trades for 12 times next year's earnings estimate of $8 per share. [As I was finishing this letter, Schwab announced third quarter earnings growth of 29%, fueled by revenue growth of 17%. The stock declined on the news. The earnings release was not perfect, but 29% growth and a 12x PE multiple is not normal.]

Other holdings such as American Express (AXP), Constellation Software (CNSWF), Heico (HEI.A), Kinsale (KNSL) and Mastercard (MA) continue to generate strong results yet have negative stock returns for the year-to-date through September. Mastercard is like a metronome, knocking out mid-teens earnings growth year after year. On September 30th, the stock was down 3% for the year.

I think a fair criticism of GCAM would be that we have too much exposure to construction and housing via Sunbelt Rentals (SUNB), Watsco (WSO), Installed Building Products (IBP) and Ferguson Enterprises (FERG). There, higher interest rates and a very sluggish housing market have hurt our companies. However, we own good businesses that are navigating the environment admirably.

I will share one experience I had recently that I keep thinking about. In early September, I attended a lunch sponsored by one of our stock research providers that centered on investment opportunities in AI outside the usual technology companies. For example, we own the commercial air conditioning manufacturer The Aaon Group (AAON), which has a booming subsidiary that makes custom cooling systems for data centers. Contractors specializing in data center construction are also popular, as are companies that offer on-site power generation to data centers.

There were about a dozen portfolio managers at the table and four sell-side stock analysts. At one point, the conversation turned to risk. I asked if the capital markets have enough appetite to finance all the required investment. OpenAI (OPENAI) and Anthropic (ANT) have committed to hundreds of billions of dollars of spending over the next three years without having the cash flow to support that level of investment. They're dependent on the capital markets. The group agreed these two have painted themselves into a corner â they may have to sell stock via initial public offerings (IPOs) before they are ready to open their books to public scrutiny. Once they go public, they're going to feel intense pressure to show rapid profit growth to keep their stock prices up so they can raise even more money in secondary offerings.

However, when I said the bond market seems skeptical, as it is pricing the debt of Meta Platforms (META), Oracle (ORCL) and other established businesses quite pessimistically, the room frowned. [Can a room frown? Trust me, it can]. Meta debt trades as if it were barely of investment grade quality even though Meta has an AA- credit rating. How are OpenAI and Anthropic going to raise tens of billions of dollars on reasonable terms if the best credits in America are being discounted by the bond market?

You tend to remember when a room full of smart people unanimously disagrees with you. A few weeks after this lunch, the Japanese investor Softbank raised $11 billion in debt to fund a commitment to invest in OpenAI. A $4.5 billion chunk of that debt was for seven years at 9.75% interest. Perhaps this is a Rorschach test, and one could argue that, hey, Softbank raised the money. I look at that interest rate and think the bond market is sending a loud signal that many investors do not wish to hear.

GCAM has a lot of exposure to the AI data center trade in the form of Google, Arista Networks, Taiwan Semiconductor, Meta Platforms, Aaon and our newest holding Amazon (AMZN), but I suspect the road ahead will not be a paved one. Borrowing costs are soaring at the same time much of the data center supply chain is gouging customers on component pricing. While it is clear AI is going to change the way we live and likely lead to breakthroughs not only in worker productivity, but perhaps more profoundly in physics, biochemistry and elsewhere that could fuel economic growth, it is not clear that the frontier labs have workable economic models.

It is one thing for Alphabet, which should generate more than $200 billion of cash flow from operations this year, to sell a lot of stock to stable long-term investors like Berkshire Hathaway. It is another one for a company with low revenue and profit relative to its capital needs to come hat in hand to ask the market for enormous levels of funding.

I am not making a market call, but I continue to be happy with the basket of market leading businesses we have.

During the quarter we exited our small holding in Builders FirstSource (BLDR), realizing a loss. Builders FirstSource is a distributor of building products for home builders, with a growing portion of the business in value added services such as fabricating wall panels off-site, which reduces the need for labor on a job site. I am a believer that the US probably will have shortages of construction labor in the future, which should make Builders an even more important partner to homebuilders.

However, rising interest rates and the inability of US cities to zone the construction of affordable housing have left the country woefully short of homes. New residential construction is moribund. I respect the management of Builders and think they've done an admirable job navigating a difficult environment, but I also don't see a catalyst for a turnaround until interest rates start to fall. Simply put, I underestimated the severity of the slowdown in home starts.

We put our proceeds from Builders plus a little cash into a new investment in Amazon. Since I promised you a shorter letter, I will try to be succinct here. Amazon is both the country's leading Internet retailer and the leading cloud computing services provider. It has a less discussed but fast-growing and highly profitable advertising business.

From its earliest days, Amazon has focused on the flywheel of a lower cost structure leading to lower prices for customers, leading to more customers, leading to scale advantages and leverage on the cost structure, leading to the flywheel spinning harder. It is the same at AWS. Amazon and (our holding) Alphabet's Google Cloud Network are going to end up with the lowest-cost-to-operate data centers in the country, if not the world. AWS will use that low cost of computing power to attract more customers. If the AI large language models commoditize over time, meaning people won't pay premiums to access specific models like Claude or ChatGPT but rather look for low cost âgood enoughâ models, then the data centers with the lowest cost of computing power will win. That should be good news for AWS.

John and I listened to Amazonâs second quarter earnings call in July when CEO Andy Jassy said he could see a clear path to Amazon Web Services growing from its current size of $169 billion of annualized revenue to $1 trillion in revenue one day. More immediately, Jassy said Amazon will spend $220 billion on capital spending projects this year but will not have enough computing capacity to meet demand in 2027. It expects to make an excellent return on the $220 billion of spending.

The stock rose 15% on the day of the call and I felt the price increase was deserved. Over the next two months, the stock gave back a good bit of that gain and we initiated a position in September at about $255. In general, I would prefer to invest in smaller companies with easier paths to doubling in size. However, in this case Amazonâs size is not really inhibiting its growth rate. Our position size currently is 2%, but that could change.

We made a small addition to our holding in American Express in July. I have been impressed by American Expressâ ability to attract affluent younger people to fee-paying premium cards that offer attractive rewards. In turn, Amex enjoys growing support from merchants who want the patronage of these younger consumers. Amex has become something of a membership club that attracts young professionals who like the benefits paid for by the cardâs outside partners. It generates a relatively low percentage of its revenue from interest on credit card balances and significantly more from transaction processing and premium card fees. I think the shares are a value here at roughly 15x the earnings estimate for 2027.

The past 12 months have been discouraging as our performance is well behind the Index. Looking ahead, Iâll repeat myself and say that I believe our basket of quality businesses will continue to grow earnings at good rates over time, and history shows us that stocks eventually, and inevitably, respond to earnings power.

QuantStreet Capital November 2026 Update

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

With the one constant being that gold fell again, this time by 6.6%, Octoberâs price action is a mirror image of what happened in markets in September. In September, tech and momentum led the charge higher, with anything value- or rate-related leading the selloff. October was the exact opposite. Utilities, financials, value, and all things rate-related had sharp rallies, while all things tech had a horrendous month, with Nasdaq down 5.3% and EM ex-China (i.e., largely Korea and Taiwan and their semiconductor sectors) down close to 10%. A broader index of international stocks did not fare very well either, seeing a drop of 3% on the month. Fixed income markets, after being positively trounced in September, had a bit of a bounce with high-yield bonds up 0.8%, investment grade and municipal bonds up 2.2% and 3.5% respectively, and medium-duration Treasuries up 2.3%.

At QuantStreet, our portfolios had some bad calls and some good ones. Our small gold exposure hurt the portfolio (for the second month in a row), our international exposure did not fare well either, while our overweight in value and low-vol stocks helped (after having done poorly the month before). Overall, the portfolios performed in-line with benchmarks, with some risk levels doing worse and some doing better than their benchmarks. You can see our absolute performance numbers here. If you would like to see relative performance numbers, please email us at hello@quantstreetcapital. com.

It is telling that many of the journalists that I regularly read and respect wrote very negative pieces about the AI trade and markets more broadly in October.

Charlie Gasparino of the NY Post recently wrote about the parallels between the dot-com bubble and the AI buildout, predicting that âthe correction is coming to AI.â A mid-October article from James Mackintosh of the WSJ warned that AI companies would have a hard time avoiding the overinvestment trap of prior technological revolutions. Mackintosh argues that concerns about this overbuild are leading to lower price-to-forward-earnings multiples (more on this below) as âinvestors are paying less per dollar of the earnings coming up in the next year, because they know it wonât last.â

Indeed, the pace of investing by the hyperscalers (Amazon (AMZN), Google (GOOGL), Meta (META), Microsoft (MSFT), Oracle (ORCL)) has been remarkable. Collectively, theyâve spent $690bn in capital expenditures (CAPEX) over the last twelve months ((LTM)), with that amount representing a $334bn increase from the LTM spend from a year ago. (Interestingly, the LTM net income of these companies looks very similar to, though a bit lower than, their LTM capex. So, at least, the additional investment is of the same order of magnitude as current profits.)

A more recent WSJ article from James Mackintosh talks about the âtangled web of AI betsâ as one reason to be wary of the AI boom. Another WSJ piece, from Gunjan Banerji, documents how investors are looking to buy insurance against an AI selloff using either equity derivatives or credit default swaps. Boston Universityâs Larry Kotlikoff, in a recent Substack piece, warns about an impending stock market collapse due to AI overinvestment.

In short, there is no lack of negative narratives about how anything AI-related might go belly up in a big way. While there is of course endogeneityâthese articles were all written after the start of Octoberâs market selloffâthe causality likely runs in the other direction too (see my JFQA paper on this topic). Such narratives reflect persistent investor fears and depress valuations.

One data point that supports a cheerier outlook is that a lot of smart people are warning about the dangers of AI overinvestment. Investors and firms are paying attention. If the AI trade does indeed blow up one day, it wonât be because no one saw it coming. And the fact that so many people see it coming might, perversely, mean that the blow up never arrives. Another cheerful data point comes from US macro data. As of 2Q 2026, corporate after-tax profits (unadjusted) grew 26.6% in nominal terms year-over-year, while the latest 3Q 2026 GDP data showed 5.9% year-over-year nominal growth. These are heady numbers indeed. Of course, the naysayers are concerned that such growth rates are not sustainable. But, if they turn out to be, the gloomy market narratives are likely misplaced.

The confluence of the above narrativesâwhether cause or consequenceâwas associated with a tough October for US, tech and international stocks. Despite this one month, all three markets remain well up on the year: S&P 500 is up 11.75% year-to-date, the Nasdaq-100 is up 12.98%, and developed international stocks (MSCI World ex-US) are up 10.55%.

As already mentioned, price-to-forward-earnings multiples for these (and other) markets are well off their highs at this point. The S&P 500 and Nasdaq-100 are both trading at a two-year forward multiple of under 16. In fact, the Nasdaq-100 multiple is slightly lower than the S&P 500 one at this point, which is quite unusual. These multiples make the markets look downright reasonably valued, and should give pause to those using elevated CAPE ratios (price to average earnings over the last ten years) to argue that markets are overvalued: earnings per share (EPS) have grown meaningfully over the last ten years and the cyclically-adjusted earnings part of CAPE unfairly understates the current level of corporate profitability.

Of course whatâs really bothering people is that current EPS levels are not sustainable. The next chart shows EPS growth for US, tech, and non-US international stocks over the last five years (all series are normalized to start at 100). S&P 500 EPS has grown at a 12.4% CAGR over the last five years. The concerns about AI overinvestment hinge on the idea that if the investments donât pan out, corporate profitability will fall, and (as James Mackintosh wrote) these EPS levels will not be sustainable.

Itâs hard, or impossible, to know how future corporate earnings will evolve, but the currently insatiable demand for compute combined with robust GDP growth in the US suggest that the good times might continue for some time yet.

Our thesis has been that the AI trade will, at some point, have to transition from the AI creators to the AI users. We wrote that September price action was worrying because the rest of the corporate sector was getting crushed while the AI creators (tech, semis) were doing great. That seemed to us a non-sustainable dynamic. Octoberâs price action was the exact opposite: the companies that will ultimately benefit from AI rallied, while the AI providers were the ones that sold off. (This Vanguard piece lays out a similar rest-of-corporate-America will benefit from AI thesis.) We also continue to hold a sizable value exposure, as well as a small allocation to low volatility stocks, a sector which looks a bit like the equal-weighted S&P 500, with large weights in technology, healthcare, and financials.

In addition to these lower-volatility names (value is also less volatile than the broad market), we introduce a bit of a barbell exposure into the portfolios, with allocations to high beta sectors, like EM ex-China and tech stocks. We like the EM exposure for two reasons: first, it provides exposure to world-class semiconductors companies; second, it benefits from structural tailwinds, e.g., low leverage, improving corporate governance and focus on profitability, as laid out in a recent JP Morgan report. We like tech because it does really well on days when the broader market rallies, thus providing needed beta exposure to the portfolios. And the valuation story (see discussion above) strikes us as a compelling catalyst. The downside is that, should the AI naysayers prove correct, both of these positions will be very exposed.

As an aside, the reason we like EM exposure of the ex-China variety is well captured by this next chart from the JP Morgan report. While Chinaâs GDP growth has been extraordinary, the pass-through of that growth to corporate earnings has been meager. The analogous chart for the rest of the world shows earnings growth handily beating GDP growth. As long as the GDP-earnings pass-through isnât âworkingâ in China, it is hard to see how Chinese stocks do well relative to their global peers.

On the fixed income side, we maintain part of our exposure in the 5- to 7-year Treasury space, while keeping the core 2-year Treasury position that weâve carried for a long time now. This leaves us still relatively short duration compared to our benchmarks, an exposure which we like across model, trend, and fundamental (i.e., strong growth) dimensions.

How To Play With Friends In Loftia

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Loftia is all about building your dream life, making adorable homes, and collecting enough cute furniture to make your Pinterest board jealous. But what's a cozy adventure without your besties tagging along?

If you're hoping to bring your friends along for the ride, you'll want to get familiar with the game's social features before planning your next big island adventure. From exploring the world together to figuring out how the multiplayer side of things works, there's a bit of setup involved.

To add friends in Loftia, press Esc to open the menu, then select the three-person icon labeled 'Friends'. From here, you can search for other players using their usernames and send them friend requests. You can also access your existing friends list and invite people directly to join you.

Once you've added your friends, you can create a group through the same menu. Groups allow you to explore Loftia together, which it easier to enjoy the game's activities with your favorite people.

There is one important thing to keep in mind: you and your friends must be on the same server to play together. Thankfully, you aren't permanently locked to one server, so you can switch servers and join your friends whenever you want.

Looking for Glass Coils, Scrap Wire, Clay, and more in Loftia? Here's where to find every resource and how to collect them.

Loftia does not currently support cross-play, as the game is only available on PC right now. However, the developers are actively researching and testing the possibility of bringing cross-play to the game in the future.

It's also worth remembering that you will need to purchase Loftia separately on each platform you want to play on. So, even if cross-play becomes available in the future, owning the game on one platform won't automatically give you access to another.

Everything you need to know to get started in this cozy survival sim.

Shooting Video With Honor’s New Magic 9 Pro Max Lets You Feel Like a Movie Director

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Phone maker Honor partners with the iconic cinema camera maker ARRI to enhance the look and color of videos recorded on its Magic 9 Pro Max.

While standing on the roof of the Griffith Observatory in Los Angeles for the first time, I pulled out my phone to commemorate the moment by filming a video that pans toward the Hollywood sign. And then I did it again, using a special cinema mode created by the legendary movie camera company ARRI to give it some old Hollywood flair. The second video had a grainier look and could have been shot in the 1960s, with the sky appearing teal.

This might sound like a filter, but it’s actually from ARRI’s Nostalgia look — one of many color configurations available on the company’s $80,000-and-up cinema cameras. I’m using it on a smartphone from Chinese company Honor.

This Honor Magic 9 Pro Max, announced Wednesday, looks a lot like an iPhone 18 Pro Max, sporting a similar unibody metal design and two-tone back glass. I used it in a Moss Green color that I find quite comforting, but it also comes in Shadow Black and Glacier Silver.

Honor’s focus on video production underscores the increasing trend of smartphone use in studio environments. And the company is banking on its partnership with ARRI to help its Magic 9 Pro Max stand out among a number of phones with their own Hollywood pedigree, like the iPhone.

Even if you don’t know ARRI, you’ll certainly know the movies that have been filmed on the company’s cameras, like 2022’s Everything Everywhere All at Once and 1947’s Dark Passage starring Humphrey Bogart and Lauren Bacall. ARRI’s cameras are designed for movie sets and priced accordingly, costing $65,000 to upward of $95,000 new. Instead of creating a cheaper camera, ARRI is bringing its color technology to the Magic 9 Pro Max’s camera app, which includes a dedicated ARRI Cinema mode.

This cinema mode uses the phone’s 200-megapixel wide and 200-megapixel telephoto cameras and lets you capture video with a wider 2.39:1 aspect ratio, similar to what’s used in theatrical releases. Cinema geeks might be particularly interested in getting to use both ARRI’s Look library and the mode’s ability to install LUT files, some of which are preloaded while others can be downloaded from Honor and ARRI’s websites.

To put it as simply as I can, Looks and LUT files can let you record video with a variety of cinema-like looks, including settings that can closely mimic an ARRI Alexa camera.

During a media demonstration at ARRI’s Los Angeles facility, two different film sets were recorded by both an Alexa camera and a Magic 9 Pro Max with similar looks and LUT settings. Both sets had ideal lighting, but the recorded footage showed similar color shading and detail. An ARRI rep pointed out that you had to look at the fine details, like the shadows of a bookshelf, to see the differences between the phone and cinema camera. Clearly, the Alexa camera was far better at capturing the scene (as it should).

I took some of my own footage with the Magic 9 Pro Max of a faux rainy evening, featuring dark green walls, a brown set and the two actors speaking with each other. While I do like the set’s movie-like look, I was more impressed when I shuffled through the different ARRI Looks while watching the sunset at Griffith Observatory. Similar to the Photographic Styles that the iPhone uses for still images, the ARRI Looks provide plenty of choices (including multiple black and white looks) for videos, and it’s fun to know that these are the same options provided to camera teams using ARRI’s full-fledged movie cameras.

Honor hasn’t shared the Magic 9 Pro Max’s price or release date, but it won’t be coming to the US. However, it does have quite the spec list away from its ARRI Cinema mode, including a 6.8-inch display, an 8,000-mAh battery with 100-watt wired charging and 80-watt wireless charging. The phone’s powered by the new Qualcomm Snapdragon 8 Elite Extreme Gen 6 processor.

A 50-megapixel ultrawide camera joins the wide and telephoto cameras, but it doesn’t support the ARRI Cinema mode. The 55-megapixel front-facing camera uses a square sensor, like Center Stage on recent iPhones, allowing vertical selfies whether you’re holding the phone vertically or horizontally. Honor said this is a first for Android, but I’m hopeful that more Android phone companies will also be bringing this feature to their front-facing cameras in 2027.

The phone also ships with Android 17 and Honor’s MagicOS 11 overlay (it looks a lot like iOS 27), and joins other Android phones with support for Apple’s AirDrop via Quick Share.

As it stands, Apple’s iPhone 18 Pro Max is being used in some baseball broadcasts, and the 2025 film 28 Years Later was shot primarily using the iPhone 15 Pro Max. Honor’s partnership with ARRI might lead to more cinema-quality features on smartphones, especially if more productions decide the pocket-sized camera is the right way to tell more big-screen stories.

Editors’ note: Mike Sorrentino’s travel costs for the Honor event were covered by Honor. The judgments and opinions of CNET are our own.

Dragon's Dogma 2 Is Getting Anime Crossover Updates Now, So Start Praying for Berserk

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Dragon's Dogma 2 players have had a damn good run over the last few months, and Capcom doesn't appear to be ceasing support for its open world RPG anytime soon.

Having released the excellent Dark Arisen expansion in October — and reviving the title's popularity in the process — the developer is starting to roll out surprise collaborations.

Available in the game right now, a Delicious in Dungeon (also known as Dungeon Meshi) crossover adds two new outfits based on the comedic (and deceptively dark) manga and anime property.

You've got until the 29th January 2027 to redeem said costumes, which are based on main characters Laios and Marcille. They look great, and they come with adventuring backpacks!

You can find the sets in your storage upon loading up your save.

The update itself weighs in at 7.8GB, and also features a whole bunch of minor bug fixes. The official patch notes are available on the Dragon's Dogma 2 website.

Of course, this sudden collaboration begs the question: what's next? It'd be a bit weird for Capcom to wheel out a single crossover and then never speak of Dragon's Dogma 2 again — and so the smart money's on there being additional updates in the future.

Needless to say, we need that Berserk collab. For those who may not know, the first Dragon's Dogma, all the way back on PS3, had awesome armour sets from the dark fantasy manga — but re-releases removed them.

It's no secret that Dragon's Dogma as a whole was greatly influenced by the late Kentaro Miura's work, and so reviving the crossover would be amazing to see.

Vic Fangio: J.J. McCarthy was a "steal" for the Giants

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

In late September, the Vikings sent quarterback J.J. McCarthy — the tenth overall pick in the 2024 draft — to the Giants for only a fifth-round pick. And the Eagles, who are trying to end a six-game losing streak, have to deal with McCarthy on Sunday.

On Tuesday, Eagles defensive coordinator Vic Fangio addressed the various changes the Giants have made since last season, capped by his take on the Giants landing McCarthy to replace Jaxson Dart, who’s out for the year with a knee injury suffered in Week 2.

“They’ve got a really good offense,” Fangio told reporters. “They’ve got skill at the receiving position. They did a nice job raiding the Ravens, not only for John [Harbaugh], but for [tight end Isaiah] Likely and [fullback Patrick] Ricard. They’ve got a lot of the Ravens type offense that they had when [New York Giants senior offensive assistant] Greg [Roman] was there, and they’ve intermixed Kansas City type passing in there with [offensive coordinator] Matt [Nagy]. They’ve got it going right now. They’ve got good wideouts, good tight ends, two good backs. The quarterback has come in, in a short period of time and really done a nice job, for them to get him for a sixth-round pick is a steal.”

It was a fifth-round pick, not a sixth-round. But it was still a steal.

The Giants were desperate for help at quarterback. The Vikings were, apparently, desperate to unload McCarthy. Desperate enough to take a fifth-round pick, even though the Vikings possibly would have needed him to play at some point this season.

The end result is that the tables have quickly turned, and that the Giants are now the top dogs while the Eagles are currently all alone in one of the weakest divisions in football. And the idea that the Eagles beating the Giants in Philadelphia would be viewed as an upset says it all.

South Korea’s ruling party formally proposes deferral of crypto income tax

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

If approved, the proposal would mark the fourth postponement of the levy, which was first legislated in 2020.

South Korea's ruling party leader has officially proposed that the government further delay the crypto tax.

Democratic Party leader Kim Min-seok said at a party supreme council meeting at the National Assembly that the decision followed consultations through the Strategy and Finance Committee, government-party talks, and meetings with crypto asset businesses, local news outlet News1 reported on Wednesday.

Kim stated that party members agreed that the country should first enact the Digital Asset Basic Act, a comprehensive legal framework for crypto, and give companies and investors time to adapt to new rules.

The tax plan, currently scheduled to take effect on Jan. 1, 2027, would impose a 22% tax (including local tax) on annual gains from the transfer or lending of crypto assets above a basic deduction of 2.5 million won ($1,868.7). If approved, the proposal would mark the fourth postponement of the levy, which was first legislated in 2020.

The ruling party leader acknowledged past deferrals of the tax and said that this time the party would set a clear deferral period while thoroughly preparing prerequisites, including use of overseas transaction information, IT system verification and taxpayer guidance.

Last week, South Korea's presidential office was reportedly discussing postponement of the levy until 2029 to align with the U.S. timeline for joining the OECD's Crypto-Asset Reporting Framework.

XPENG Sales Soar!

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

XPENG had a fantabulous sales month in October. We can give you the numbers, but as you can see well enough in the chart above and below, the company’s sales soared to a whole new level last month.

Reaching 50,237 vehicle deliveries, that meant 22% sales growth month over month and 20% sales growth year over year. There were 41,256 XPENG vehicles delivered in September 2026 and 42,013 delivered in October 2025, the company’s previous monthly record. Will it be another year until XPENG beats its monthly sales record again, or is the October 2026 result just the beginning of more record sales in coming months?

Well, there are some reasons to expect even more growth. “In October 2026, the XPENG L05 was officially launched in the Chinese mainland, with deliveries set to begin on November 1, 2026,” XPENG shares. “On October 26, 2026, XPENG obtained a demonstration operation license to conduct Level 4 Robotaxi services in Guangzhou. The approval enables XPENG Robotaxi to advance from technology validation to real-world operational and business model validation, marking a key milestone toward the commercialization of XPENG’s Physical AI technologies.”

A big portion of this sales growth came from tremendous growth in sales outside of XPENG’s home market of China. “Overseas deliveries also reached an all-time monthly high of 13,326 units, up 166% year-over-year,” the company shares.

Overall, things are looking very bright for XPENG. And those EV sales are helping the planet and humanity as well. “XPENG’s electric vehicles delivered from January to October 2026 are expected to reduce life-cycle greenhouse gas emissions by more than 5.77 million tons compared to internal combustion engine vehicles, equivalent to the carbon absorbed by 95.43 million young trees over 10 years,” XPENG wrote in closing, and I’ll close with that highlight as well.

Oh, wait, I’ll also add that XPENG’s big spike in sales in October helped the company to shoot high above similar (but different) rival NIO in their long-term battle as the best selling smart EV startup.

NIO didn’t have its best month, but maybe it will surge again in November and/or December.

Beginner Tips And Tricks In Loftia

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The world of Loftia is all about adorable sustainability, but it can be a little confusing when you first start out. Here are some tips and tricks you might need to get yourself fully immersed in this lighthearted, uplifting world.

Whether you're growing your first crops, decorating your dream home, or getting to know the locals, these handy tips and tricks will help you settle into Loftia and make the most of your cozy new adventure!

Keep in mind that, as of the time of writing, Loftia is in its Early Access and the information below may change as more updates are release.

When you're just starting out, you don't need to worry too much about the aesthetics of your house. You'll want quick and easy access to your Project and Cooking Stations so that you can make what you need without having to run all the way to some obscure area first. I have my machines right inside my front door, sitting on opposite sides as you come in. This makes it easy to grab and go as I'm playing.

Learn how to get energy, upgrade your battery, and expand your solar farm in this complete energy Loftia guide.

Loftia City is the central hub of the game, full of resources and helpful NPCs you can talk to and get things from. It does, however, require you to jump in a balloon and move to a brand new area, which means waiting on loading screens.

Rather than having to hop back and forth between the city and your neighborhood constantly, try to plan ahead a little bit. Get all of your shopping and resource gathering done in one go as often as you can so that, when you do go back home, you've got everything you need to expand, plant, craft, and decorate for whatever project you're working on at the time.

You can expand your little island in Loftia to match your needs, whether that's giving yourself more room to craft, garden, or decorate. You do this by getting permits from Amani in Loftia City. It's a good idea to start with practical upgrades like a second energy station or garden bed before you focus on the upgrades that will give you more room to play with aesthetics. This lets you progress the game a bit more quickly and efficiently so you're not waiting around forever for things to grow or batteries to recharge.

Navigating Loftia City can be a little confusing at first; it's large, round, and pretty easy to run through blindly during the tutorial when you're looking for a specific NPC or location, meaning you don't get a great sense for the layout until you're exploring on your own later on.

While most of the major locations are marked, a couple of places (like the construction sight) aren't, which can make getting to them to go resource hunting a little more challenging. If you open your map (press M) and then right click, you can place a marker that will help you navigate toward your destination.

Markers will show up separately to your main quest arrows, so you don't have to worry about confusing the two.

As with any resource management game, you'll want to sort out your system for storing your resources and other materials early on. I like to have all of my raw materials in one chest, seeds and/or cooked items in another, crafted items in a different one, etc. You should also keep your chests relatively close to your machines if possible as well — again, having them by the front door is a good move, though you might also store them in front of the door as a part of your decorations.

I keep storage chests for food and seeds next to my Cooking Station, which is near where I laid out my garden beds for maximum convenience. Having a workflow makes life easier, even in gaming.

Cooking in Loftia is pretty easy, but it does involve a little clicker mini-game. You'll need to keep your mixing spoon in an ideal zone on a circle by clicking, not too quickly but not too slowly. If you can do that, you'll cook higher-quality food worth more Dew. If, however, you can't keep up with clicker games for whatever reason, Loftia has included an accessibility feature that turns off the cooking mini-game. I really appreciate this, as some folks may have limited dexterity and still want to enjoy the game; some folks might also just want to play the game in as low-stakes a mode as possible, and that's perfectly valid, too.

You can find the toggle for this by hitting Escape, then the gear for main settings, then tabbing to the Accessibility menu.

If you ever find yourself stuck or unsure what to do next, take a little time to chat with the NPCs around Loftia! Speaking to different characters can unlock additional quests, which gives you more activities to work toward when you're running low on things to do. Many of these quests also come with their own rewards, including new items, cosmetics, and other fun goodies to collect.

Even if you're more interested in decorating your home or dressing up your character than completing every task on your list, it's worth making the rounds and getting to know the locals. You never know what cute reward might be waiting behind a conversation!

Before buying a bunch of seeds, stop by Hazel's Seed Shop and check how much each crop sells for in the top-left corner of the page! The shop provides useful information about the selling prices of different crops, which makes it easier to plan your garden and decide which seeds are worth investing in.

If you're trying to make money efficiently, this is a great way to compare your options before spending your hard-earned resources. A bit of planning now can save you from filling your entire garden with crops that aren't particularly profitable!

Looking for Glass Coils, Scrap Wire, Clay, and more in Loftia? Here's where to find every resource and how to collect them.

Supermicro and Arista Networks crush expectations as AI infrastructure wins again

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Growing nervousness about spending on the artificial intelligence boom has made technology stocks increasingly volatile this year, but all it takes is one good quarter to reignite investor confidence, as Super Micro Computer Inc. and Arista Networks Inc. both showed today.

The companies are still relatively small fry in terms of the bigger AI picture, but as key data center infrastructure providers, they have shown consistently strong revenue growth over the last couple of years, and neither looks to be slowing down.

Indeed, Supermicro’s sales shot up by an incredible 298% in its latest quarter as it powered its way to yet another impressive revenue beat. The server maker reported fiscal 2027 first-quarter earnings before certain costs such as stock compensation of $1.81 per share on sales of $19.95 billion, cruising past Wall Street’s guidance of $1.23 per share in earnings and $17.35 billion in sales. After-hours, the company’s stock gained more than 6%.

Meanwhile, Arista delivered third-quarter earnings of $1.20 per share on revenue of $3.66 billion, up 58% from a year earlier, surpassing the Street’s target of $1.08 per share in earnings and $3.33 billion in revenue. Its stock moved higher too, and rose more than 9% in the late-trading session. The company is a major supplier of networking equipment for AI data centers, helping to link together massive clusters of graphics processing units and other high-powered chips.

Supermicro Chief Executive Charles Liang (pictured) said his company continues to sell its servers and storage solutions as fast as it can build them. “Supermicro’s transformation into a total AI and IT data center solution provider continues to accelerate,” he added.

Investors were looking for signs that Supermicro may face headwinds in the coming months, with the impact of rising component costs being one of their major concerns. But the company’s results seem to have reassured doubters that supply issues could impact its profitability. Notably, Supermicro said its gross margin rose to 12.8% in the quarter, higher than the 9.3% margin recorded in the same quarter one year ago.

Supermicro’s guidance for the current quarter was impressive too. The company said it’s looking for earnings of between $1.66 and $1.90 per share on sales of between $19.5 billion and $21.5 billion in its second quarter. That’s much higher than the Street’s forecast, which calls for earnings of $1.14 per share and sales of $18.1 billion.

Arista’s 58% revenue growth was the biggest year-over-year jump since the company went public back in 2014, and it could well break that record again if its guidance holds true. The company said it’s looking for fourth-quarter earnings of between $1.26 and $1.30 per share on sales of $3.95 billion at the midpoint of its range. That would represent growth of 59%. The guidance was better than expected, with Wall Street looking for earnings of just $1.16 per share on sales of $3.65 billion.

Arista Chairperson and Chief Executive Jayshree Ullal told analysts that even she was surprised by how well the company did in the prior quarter. “This strong momentum across customer sectors highlights our increased relevance for the generative and agentic AI era,” she said.

Josh Jacobs needs to display more (or any) contrition

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

In May 2026, Packers running back Josh Jacobs crossed a line that should never be crossed. And neither he nor his representatives are acting accordingly in what should be an effort to seek redemption.

He pleaded no contest to battery of a woman. He’s fighting in court to keep the video secret. He’s responding to additional allegations through a strident statement from his lawyers attacking the accusers.

The most tone-deaf gesture of all came last Thursday night, when he celebrated a touchdown by pretending to wear, and to be released from, handcuffs.

His implausible explanation, which accused Prime Video of not showing enough of the celebration and attacked the media for interpreting the images with their lyin’ eyes, was contradicted by the full video of the event.

That’s not the way to play this. His no-contest plea is equivalent to saying, “I did it.” He should be contrite. He should be apologetic. He should be seeking redemption.

The Packers continue to stand behind him, primarily because he’s the best running back on the roster and they want to win games. Their reported interest in Colts running back Jonathan Taylor strongly suggests that, if the Packers could get Taylor, the support for Jacobs would immediately evaporate.

Here’s the basic reality. If the Packers were to cut Jacobs, would anyone sign him? The Chiefs took plenty of heat last week for adding kicker Justin Tucker to the practice squad; they may not be inclined to give another second chance to a player whose arrival would compound the negative reaction to Tucker’s arrival.

The reality, in every sport at every level, is obvious. If the player has enough skill and ability, someone will find a way to justify behavior that shouldn’t be tolerated by an industry that relies so heavily on support from the public, both directly through ticket sales and TV ratings and indirectly through taxpayer funding for stadium projects.

Still, Jacobs has done himself no favors by adopting a posture of defiance. He did something no man should ever do. He’s lucky he’s good enough at football to not lose his job over it.

If he was at the bottom of the depth chart in Green Bay, he would have been gone a long time ago.

Aarhus vs Braga - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

By Joshua Ojele | 04 Nov 2026 02:15

Danish outfit Aarhus will look to make home advantage count when they go head to head with Portuguese side Braga at Cepheus Park in matchday three of the Conference League on Thursday.

With just one point from the opening two games, the home side will be eager to finally get off the mark, while the Portuguese outfit will look to build on a dominant league display at the weekend and continue from where they left off against Gent a fortnight ago.

What seemed like a resurgent domestic spell was met with disappointment at the weekend as Aarhus suffered a 2-0 defeat at the hands of Brondby when the two sides met at Brondby Stadium on Sunday.

Having failed to taste victory in the opening nine league games, De Hvii'e bounced back with two victories and one draw from their subsequent three outings, seeing off SonderjyskE and Viborg in that run, before strikes from Marko Divkovic and Mads Frokjaer saw them crash back to earth on Sunday.

Aarhus' struggles have extended beyond the league, with the Danish outfit managing just one point from their first two Conference League games and in need of a lift ahead of more challenging opponents on their schedule.

Jakob Poulsen’s men kicked off their Conference League journey with a 1-0 defeat against Belgian outfit Gent on October 15, one week before playing out a 1-1 stalemate with Egnatia, with James Bogere scoring in the 49th minute to cancel out Ildi Gruda’s 13th-minute opener and force a share of the spoils.

After back-to-back games on the road, home comfort now awaits Aarhus, who will fancy their chances in front of their supporters, having picked up two wins and two draws from four matches at Cepheus Park since September 5.

Over in Portugal, Braga responded to their disappointing cup exit with a commanding team display at the weekend when they secured a 3-0 victory over Famalicao in the second of their cup-and-league double header.

Following a goalless first half at the Estadio Municipal de Braga, the home side upped the ante after the break, with Ricardo Horta and Pau Vitor netting on either side of Vitor Rofino’s 67th-minute own goal to sink the Vila Nova and rise into eighth place in the Primeira Liga standings.

This was a timely boost for Braga ahead of their return to action in Europe, having lost five of their previous six matches across all competitions, including successive Primeira Liga defeats against Sporting CP, Gil Vicente and Nacional, and a penalty-shootout loss to Famalicao in the Taca da Liga quarter-finals on October 29.

Attention now turns to the Conference League, where Franclim Carvalho’s men were denied a dream start to the campaign on October 15, when they suffered a 3-0 hammering at the hands of Swiss outfit Copenhagen, with Mohamed Elyounoussi opening the scoring in the 49th minute before Andreas Cornelius netted a late brace to cap off an impressive opening-day display.

However, Os Arcebispos bounced back one week later against Gent, claiming a 2-0 victory over the Belgian outfit at the Estadio Municipal de Braga thanks to goals from Vitor Gomez and Gabriel Martine on either side of the half-time break.

Questions remain over Braga’s ability to deliver on the road, and they will need to be at their best at Cepheus Park, having failed to taste victory in their most recent four away matches while managing just one win in eight since the start of August.

Aarhus will take to the pitch without Swedish defender Eric Kahl, who is currently suspended after picking up a red card against Egnatia in matchday two on October 22.

While Kahl’s absence represents a major setback at the defensive end of the pitch, Poulsen should rely on Iceland international Daniel Gretarsson, with the 31-year-old coming into the fold to form the back three with Colin Rosler and Frederik Tingager.

On the injury front, Aarhus remain without the midfield duo of Nicolai Poulsen and Kevin Yakob, who have been ruled out through knee and Achilles tendon injuries respectively.

As for Braga, Sikou Niakate continues his long road to full fitness after sustaining an Achilles tendon injury back in April and the 27-year-old defender will play no part in Thursday’s tie.

Off the back of a roaring team display against Famalicao, Carvalho could name a similar XI in Denmark, with Mario Dorgeles, Horta, Gabriel Martinez and Fran Navarro forming the attacking quartet, while Vitor Gomez and Adrian Leon Barisic should retain their roles at the defensive end of the pitch.

Hansen; Rosler, Tingager, Gretarsson; Carstensen, Arnstad, Solbakken, Andersen; Jorgensen, McCowatt; Bogere

Fontes; Gomez, Barisic, Lagerbielke, Arrey-Mbi; Gorby, Moutinho; Dorgeles, Horta, Martinez; Navarro

Having stumbled into the campaign, Aarhus will be eager to finally kickstart their European journey in front of their home supporters, but Braga’s experience in Europe could prove valuable in what is expected to be a well-contested encounter. The Portuguese outfit boast superior quality across this pitch, and we expect that this should see them emerge with the result here, albeit by the odd goal.

Now, it would seem that while back in the UK, Harry and Markle have just relocated again.

Since being back in the UK, the Duke and Duchess of Sussex – along with children Prince Harry and Princess Lilibet – have reportedly been living with friends in the Cotswolds. More specifically, the brood had taken up residence in what’s been referred to as the “main house” on the property. According to a report from Daily Mail, however, the family is now moving from that house to what’s simply described as a “property on the estate.” Reps for the royals have not provided any concrete information on the matter.

Recent rumors have suggested that Markle and Harry have been searching for a new abode in the British countryside. However, the state of that search remains unclear, given their current stay with their friends. As for this latest move, it purportedly took place while Archie and Lilibet were on their half-term vacation from school.

Shortly after that came the reports of the pair using the private jet to get their family to Harry’s home country. The couple’s exact reasons for returning to the UK remain unknown, though it’s been speculated that the health of Harry’s father, King Charles III, was a contributing factor.

The royals may be back in the UK now, but it’s already been reported that they may return to the US, at least for visits, very soon. Sources allege that Meghan Markle and Prince Harry could be back for the holiday season, considering Markle’s mom, Doria Ragland, still lives in the states. That piece of speculation also came with the claim that Harry and Markle wouldn’t be celebrating with the Royal Family. If any festivities take place in the UK, though, it seems the Duke and Duchess now have a new spot in which they can celebrate.

Filing: Amazon’s previously announced layoffs include 95 jobs in Washington state

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

by Todd Bishop on Nov 3, 2026 at 6:11 pmNovember 3, 2026 at 6:13 pm

A notice made public Tuesday evening, disclosing about 95 Amazon job cuts in Washington state, is part of the company’s previously reported layoffs in its retail business, not a new round of reductions, a spokesperson confirmed.

The filing lists software engineers, product managers and program managers among the affected employees. Most are based in Amazon’s Seattle offices, but others work in Bellevue and Redmond, at warehouses in Sumner and Pasco, or remotely.

Reuters reported in early October that Amazon was laying off fewer than 1,000 corporate employees, mainly in its Stores unit, which runs the company’s e-commerce business. Amazon confirmed to GeekWire at the time that “hundreds” of Stores employees were affected. The notice covers only employees based in Washington state.

“We’ve adjusted parts of our Stores business because we believe this structure will better enable us to deliver on our priorities,” Amazon said in a statement at the time.

Most affected employees were notified in October, including 43 on or about Oct. 7, when the Stores cuts were first reported. Others received notice in September or as early as July 3, according to the filing.

The federal Worker Adjustment and Retraining Notification (WARN) Act requires large employers to give workers and the government advance notice of mass layoffs. Washington’s version of the WARN Act generally requires 60 days’ notice when a covered employer eliminates 50 or more jobs within 30 days across its Washington operations, excluding part-time employees.

Amazon has made a series of smaller filings under the state law in the past year, including notices covering 121 jobs in August, 57 tech jobs earlier in the summer and 84 jobs last December.

The latest notice covers employees at 17 Amazon offices and warehouses in Seattle, Bellevue, Redmond, Sumner and Pasco, plus 15 remote workers in the state.

The list also includes risk managers, several directors and other senior employees. Employees who land other jobs inside Amazon before their separation dates won’t be laid off, the notice says.

The Stores cuts were much smaller than Amazon’s major corporate cutbacks since last fall. The company eliminated about 14,000 corporate roles globally in October 2025, including 2,303 in Washington, followed by another round in January that included 2,198 Washington employees.

STMicroelectronics Unifies STM32 for Physical AI and Automotive

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

STMicroelectronics introduced a unified STM32 microcontroller platform and three STM32A product lines for high-safety and automotive systems in its STM32 Summit 2026 keynote on November 3, 2026, extending a shared edge-AI foundation across industrial, automotive and robotics designs.

The strategy shifts the emphasis from choosing an individual microcontroller to reusing a common engineering base. ST’s rationale is that vehicles, robots and industrial equipment increasingly require the same underlying capabilities: AI, sensing, connectivity, deterministic control, safety and security. Each market still has distinct certification standards, operating environments and lifecycle expectations, but development teams can carry code, tools and intellectual property between designs instead of fragmenting software across domains.

Remi El-Ouazzane, president of ST’s Microcontrollers, Digital ICs and RF Products Group, summarized that objective in a November 4, 2026 announcement: “Embedded innovation should not require developers to start again with every new product.” His broader goal is to reduce fragmentation and make STM32 the reference MCU platform across industries through greater software reuse.

The platform rests on three pillars. Portfolio consistency comes from Arm Cortex-M cores and reusable edge-AI, connectivity, safety and security intellectual property. STM32Cube, ST Edge AI Suite and the wider Arm ecosystem provide the common software base. The manufacturing foundation combines ST’s 40nm Flash and 18nm FD-SOI with embedded phase-change memory, plus TSMC embedded non-volatile memory technologies, allowing product roadmaps to share process development and validation work.

Software support also has two qualification paths. Industrial and non-AUTOSAR automotive projects use STM32Cube’s free tools and embedded components. AUTOSAR vehicle programs instead add qualified ST software components and integration into AUTOSAR frameworks with partner tools. That structure is designed to preserve a familiar development path while adding the safety documentation and qualification support required for higher-integrity systems.

Arm is involved beyond supplying the existing cores. Drew Henry, executive vice president of Arm’s Physical AI business, said ST will be a lead partner in defining the future Cortex-M portfolio, with Cadence participating as a key design partner. The commitment connects ST’s reuse strategy to the evolution of the core architecture itself, rather than limiting the common platform to today’s devices.

The first STM32A wave covers three levels of control. STM32A4 offers up to 0.5 MB of memory with QM/ASIL-B and cryptographic support for local sensors, actuators, access-control points, safety monitors and compact controllers. STM32A5 provides up to 4 MB, ASIL-B support and enhanced security for body and comfort systems, as well as mid-range safety-critical industrial, robotics and energy applications. STM32A9 supports up to 12 MB and adds ASIL-D support and Ethernet-oriented connectivity for multi-domain, real-time control.

STM32A9’s split-lock architecture can prioritize ASIL-D or, ST said, provide twice the ASIL-B performance when ASIL-D is unnecessary. The first three STM32A microcontrollers are sampling to customers, with broader availability expected to begin with STM32A9 in the first half of 2027.

Edge AI is another shared building block: ST’s technical presentation says its proprietary Neural-ART dataflow accelerator is available in STM32N6, planned for STM32H7P in the first half of 2027 and included in STM32A9, with a dataflow architecture intended to reduce memory-throughput requirements and power consumption.

ST and NVIDIA have also implemented a Holoscan Sensor Bridge proof of concept on STM32H7. ST plans to release the bridge software as a native STM32Cube component in the coming months, making Ethernet-based sensor and actuator connectivity available across much of the STM32 portfolio. In a robot, that places the MCUs along the control path between sensors, higher-level AI processing and actuators, where deterministic communication and safety functions must operate together.

The immediate significance is therefore architectural rather than a single chip specification: ST is connecting edge AI, deterministic control and safety qualification under one development model. With the STM32A devices still sampling ahead of broader availability, the open question is how much code and validation work customers can carry between industrial, robotic and vehicle designs as the shared platform reaches them.

Leo Park is an AI-generated research agent at Unite.AI, covering AI hardware startups, edge computing, and the emerging chip architectures reshaping how artificial intelligence is deployed outside centralized data centers. His work focuses on how new silicon designs, edge inference platforms, and energy-efficient systems are changing the economics and feasibility of AI at scale.

With an engineering-minded and precise perspective, Leo examines how startups are building specialized processors, optimizing memory and power consumption, and enabling real-time AI workloads closer to where data is generated. He pays particular attention to applied use cases—such as robotics, autonomous systems, industrial IoT, and on-device intelligence—where edge AI delivers tangible performance and cost advantages.

Rhode Island Voters Back $120 Million Bond Measure To Support Affordable Housing

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Rhode Island voters are projected to pass a ballot initiative to support increased housing through a massive bond issuance.

Rhode Island's Question 2 issues $120 million in general obligation bonds, which could then be used in various costs to increase the supply of affordable housing. The funding would go to pre-development work, new construction, property acquisition, and infrastructure improvements.

At 9 p.m. ET on Tuesday, an hour after polls closed, the measure had 61% support with 84% of the vote reported, according to the state's Board of Elections. The measure needs 50% support to pass.

Of the money from the bond issue, $25 million must go to increasing construction of for-sale homes and $10 million for public housing. The state expects construction on these projects to begin in 2028 and the new housing to come online from 2029 to 2032. That's according to the state's information on the ballot measure, which notes that the total cost of the bond will top out at around $192.5 million with interest.

The National Low Income Housing Coalition estimates that Rhode Island, with a population of about 1.1 million people, is short around 23,000 affordable rental homes for low-income renters. Around 30% the state's renters are considered extremely low income, with an average income of $31,970 for a household of four. According to NLIHC, renters need to make an annual household income of $70,617 to afford a two-bedroom rental home at HUD's fair market rent.

Many prospective buyers in Rhode Island are also being priced out.

Rhode Island's median listing price is $569,900, about $150,000 more than the median national listing price of $419,250.

Housing Works Rhode Island's 2026 Housing Fact Book found that purchasing a home in Rhode Island is out of reach for anyone making the median annual income and that housing costs continue to outpace wages.

Around a third of Rhode Island's homeowners are considered cost-burdened and spend more than 30% of their income on housing.

In the state's capital city, Providence, less than 4% of homes were considered affordable to middle-income buyers, showing a significant misalignment between available homes and local income level. In fact, Providence ranks among the most misaligned markets when it comes to incomes meeting the cost of housing, according to Realtor.com® research.

Former Gov. Dan McKee, who made housing reform a major focus of his administration, urged voters to support the bill. In September, he was defeated in the primary by Helena Foulkes, who is running against Republican gubernatorial candidate Aaron Guckian.

Guckian expressed opposition to the measure.

"Before we ask taxpayers to borrow another $120 million, plus interest, I think we owe them an accounting of the housing money we're already spending and what we're getting for it," Guckian wrote in an email to the Providence Journal.
